511	\subsection{Selective Gradient Stopping and Gradient Decomposition}
512	\label{sec:sgs_gradient_analysis}
513	
514	\subsubsection{Gradient Decomposition Results}
515	\label{sec:gradient_decomposition}
516	
517	\begin{figure}[t]
518	\centering
519	\includegraphics[width=0.75\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_gradient_decomposition.pdf}
520	\caption{Gradient contribution to $e_{\Delta t}$ by loss component at two training checkpoints. The reward head contributes the largest share (38--49\%) yet reward-only SGS (exp\_016e) fails; the observation decoder channel (16--22\%) carries the critical $\dt$-specific spatial information that drives the RSSM fidelity gap.}
521	\label{fig:gradient_decomp}
522	\end{figure}
523	
524	Exp\_016e collapses in the same qualitative pattern as the unmodified control: peak evaluation 326.7 at 75K steps followed by catastrophic decline to 31.7 at 95K, with NVE reaching $10.42\times$. The SGS intervention neither prevented nor attenuated CIVO.
525	
526	To explain this null result, we performed a gradient decomposition analysis using \texttt{torch.autograd.grad} to measure each loss component's contribution to $\nabla_\theta e_{\Delta t}$, where $\theta$ denotes the temporal embedding parameters. Table~\ref{tab:gradient_decomposition} reports the results.
527	
528	\begin{table}[ht]
529	\centering
530	\caption{Gradient contributions to the temporal embedding $e_{\Delta t}$ by loss component, measured via \texttt{torch.autograd.grad} with training configuration \texttt{gate\_fixed\_dt=2}. Gradient norms are $\ell_2$ norms of $\nabla_\theta \mathcal{L}_i$ for each loss $\mathcal{L}_i$.}
531	\label{tab:gradient_decomposition}
532	\small
533	\begin{tabular}{lrrrr}
534	\toprule
535	& \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{@55K}} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{\textbf{@112K}} \\
536	\cmidrule(lr){2-3} \cmidrule(lr){4-5}
537	Loss component & Share & (\%) & Share & (\%) \\
538	\midrule
539	Reward head & --- & 49.0 & --- & 38.1 \\
540	KL dynamics & --- & 34.7 & --- & 40.4 \\
541	Observation decoders & --- & 16.3 & --- & 21.5 \\
542	\bottomrule
543	\end{tabular}
544	\end{table}
545	
546	The gradient decomposition reveals a counterintuitive structure: the reward head provides the largest gradient contribution (49.0\% at 55K, 38.1\% at 112K), yet reward-only SGS (exp\_016e) fails catastrophically (NVE $10.42\times$). The observation decoder channel contributes the smallest share (16.3\% at 55K, 21.5\% at 112K) yet carries the critical $\dt$-specific spatial information.  This dissociation between gradient magnitude and causal importance explains why exp\_016e, which targets the largest pathway, is functionally insufficient for CIVO prevention.
547	
548	The gradient structure is relatively stable across training (reward shifts from 49\% to 38\%, obs from 16\% to 22\%, KL from 35\% to 40\%), unlike what a magnitude-based account would predict. The reward head's large gradient contribution reflects the scalar reward prediction task's sensitivity to temporal embedding parameters, but this gradient does not encode $\Delta t$-specific structural information about spatial dynamics. The observation decoder channel, despite its smaller magnitude, is the only pathway that encodes how the RSSM should differentially predict multi-dimensional proprioceptive states across temporal scales.
549	
550	\paragraph{Why the smallest channel is causally critical.} The observation decoder is the only loss component whose gradients encode how the RSSM should differentially predict multi-dimensional proprioceptive states ($14$ orientations, $9$ velocities, $1$ height) across temporal scales.  This $\dt$-specific spatial information shapes the latent state representation in ways that propagate to reward prediction: the RSSM fidelity gap ($2.78\times$ reward MAE ratio, Table~\ref{tab:rssm_fidelity}) arises because the temporal embedding learns to produce latent states that are spatially biased at $\dt{>}1$, not because the reward head directly learns scale-dependent biases.  Full-SGS targets this causal chain by preventing observation-decoder gradients from updating $e_{\Delta t}$, though its 0/2 CIVO elimination rate on Walker (seed~1: NVE reduced from $4.12\times$ to $1.66\times$ but remains CIVO-positive; seed~2: collapsed) indicates that breaking the gradient pathway alone does not fully eliminate the vulnerability.
551	
552	\subsubsection{Implications for the CIVO Mechanism}
553	\label{sec:sgs_implications}
554	
555	The gradient decomposition reveals that gradient magnitude and causal importance are decoupled in CIVO.
556	
557	\paragraph{Gradient importance $\neq$ gradient magnitude.} The reward head contributes the largest gradient share (38--49\%) to $e_{\Delta t}$, yet reward-only SGS fails.  The observation decoder contributes the smallest share (16--22\%), yet its removal (via Full-SGS) is necessary for CIVO mitigation.  This dissociation arises because the reward head's gradient, while large, does not encode $\dt$-specific structural information: it adjusts $e_{\Delta t}$ to minimize a scalar prediction error that is largely $\dt$-invariant.  The observation decoder's gradient, though smaller, encodes how the RSSM should differentially reconstruct 24-dimensional proprioceptive states across temporal scales. This is the channel through which $e_{\Delta t}$ learns scale-dependent latent biases.
558	
559	\paragraph{Reframing the intervention target.} Any gradient-level strategy that targets the reward head alone (reward prediction regularization, reward-conditioned value clipping, or reward-only SGS) targets the largest pathway but not the informationally critical one.  The failure of exp\_016e (NVE $10.42\times$) despite detaching the dominant gradient source confirms this.  Full-pathway SGS (exp\_018) detaches $e_{\Delta t}$ from all non-KL gradients, retaining only the KL dynamics contribution (34--40\%).  However, this intervention eliminates CIVO in neither seed: seed~1 shows partial mitigation (eval 448.3, peak NVE $1.66\times$, still CIVO-positive) while seed~2 collapses (eval 40.8). Removing the observation decoder gradient channel is thus contributory but not sufficient. Figure~\ref{fig:civo_decomposition} decomposes evaluation returns across conditions.
560	
561	\begin{figure}[ht]
562	\centering
563	\includegraphics[width=0.75\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_civo_decomposition.pdf}
564	\caption{CIVO decomposition: peak and final (105K step) evaluation returns on Walker Walk across four conditions. \textbf{Baseline} (001): vanilla DreamerV3. \textbf{Trained dt-emb} (008d)\protect\footnotemark: learned $d{=}32$ temporal embedding with multi-rate data. \textbf{Full-SGS} (s1, s2): trained embedding with all non-KL gradients detached from $e_{\dt}$. Trained $\dt$-embedding degrades peak eval by $73\%$ and final eval by $86\%$. Full-SGS seed~1 shows partial mitigation ($-51\%$ final, NVE $1.66\times$, still CIVO-positive) while seed~2 collapses ($-96\%$ final). CIVO elimination rate $= 0/2$.}
565	\label{fig:civo_decomposition}
566	\end{figure}
567	\footnotetext{Experiment 008d terminated at 55K steps; the 105K data point uses continuation run 009e under identical configuration.}
568	
569	Cross-environment validation on Cheetah Run (exp\_021) further clarifies the role of Full-SGS.  The intervention did not prevent the initial CIVO onset: NVE peaked at $9.94\times$ at 45K (comparable to the unmitigated exp\_010b's $8.27\times$).  However, unlike exp\_010b, which collapsed permanently to eval 51.7, exp\_021 self-corrected within ${\sim}$10K steps, with sustained NVE dropping to $0.34\times$ (55K--105K) and final evaluation recovering to 221.5.  This transient self-correcting trajectory is not attributable to the SGS intervention: the initial CIVO onset and peak severity are comparable to the unmitigated case, and the self-correction may reflect Cheetah Run's simpler dynamics rather than SGS efficacy.  Performance remains at 38.7\% of baseline (572.5), likely because Full-SGS over-aggressively blocks actor gradients (actor gradient norms near zero throughout training).  Combined with exp\_018's Walker results (0/2 CIVO elimination), these findings yield a 0/3 elimination rate across environments, indicating that gradient-level interventions alone do not eliminate the CIVO vulnerability. Additional mechanisms (e.g., forward-pass conditioning effects, residual capacity-dependent instabilities) operate beyond the gradient pathway.
570	
571	\paragraph{Connection to RSSM fidelity analysis.} The trained-embedding condition (exp\_008d) shows a $2.78\times$ reward gap between $\Delta t{=}1$ and $\Delta t{=}2$ transitions (Table~\ref{tab:rssm_fidelity}).  This gap arises because the observation decoder gradients shape $e_{\Delta t}$ to produce latent states that are spatially biased at $\dt{>}1$, and the reward head inherits these biases \textit{through the latent state} rather than learning them directly.  The reward head's large gradient contribution reflects sensitivity to $e_{\Delta t}$'s parameters but not causal responsibility for the fidelity gap.
572	
573	\subsubsection{Entropy Dynamics Across Configurations}

583	\subsubsection{Exp\_016e Reinterpreted as Stochastic Variation}
584	\label{sec:016e_reinterp}
585	
586	The gradient decomposition establishes that exp\_016e targets the largest gradient pathway (reward: 38--49\%) but not the informationally critical one (observation decoders: 16--22\%).  The apparent timing differences between exp\_016e and exp\_008d (later collapse onset, 75K vs.\ 25K, and higher peak evaluation, 326.7 vs.\ 167.2) must be attributed to stochastic variation rather than SGS effects, since the critical observation decoder channel remains intact.  All three trajectories (exp\_008d, exp\_009b, exp\_016e) share the invariant identified by the NVE diagnostic: each crosses the $1.5\times$ pathological threshold at some point during training.
587

614	
615	All seven CIVO-positive seeds cross the $\nve > 1.5\times$ threshold (Table~\ref{tab:post_civo}), yet the downstream damage is stochastic.\footnote{Exp\_009e was excluded from multi-seed analyses due to a seed configuration error (default seed=0, identical to exp\_008d).}  Full collapse is the majority outcome (6/7 seeds), with one self-correction case (Walker seed 2); seed 5 exemplifies a dissociation where NVE self-corrects ($4.72\times \to 1.22\times$) while the policy remains collapsed (eval 10.5\% of baseline). Cheetah seeds exhibit more extreme overestimation (peak NVE $8.27$--$108.52\times$ vs.\ Walker's $1.53$--$4.62\times$), consistent with the Cheetah reward scale amplifying the fidelity gap.  We hypothesize that the divergence relates to the critic's representational capacity at the time of CIVO onset.  \citet{lyle2022capacity} demonstrate that deep RL networks suffer progressive capacity loss, in which early-learned features become increasingly difficult to overwrite.  If the critic's capacity is already partially consumed when CIVO injects overestimation, the network cannot recalibrate (seed~0); if sufficient capacity remains, the critic can absorb transient overestimation and recover (seed~2).
616	
617	\paragraph{A fourth trajectory: CIVO-induced persistent value collapse.}
618	Obs-decoder SGS seed~3 (exp\_025) exhibits a trajectory not observed in the untreated seeds: healthy through 105K ($\nve < 1.0\times$, eval 818.6), CIVO onset at 135K ($\nve = 5.47\times$, eval 67.0), followed by evaluation recovery (590.2 at 215K, 64\% of baseline) but persistent value collapse ($\nve = 0.14\times$, value mean 80.6). The critic undershoots after the CIVO episode, suggesting that CIVO-induced representational damage persists even when the policy partially recovers. This contrasts with transient CIVO (seed~2, 009b), where both value and evaluation recover jointly, and with full collapse (seed~1, 008d), where neither recovers. Seed~4 exhibits the same terminal value collapse ($\nve = 0.14\times$ at 215K, eval 242), suggesting this overcorrection is systematic rather than seed-specific. The pattern adds a fourth post-CIVO outcome---policy recovery with critic damage---to the three documented in Table~\ref{tab:post_civo}.
619

621

The taxonomy reveals that CIVO is not a single failure mode but a family of related pathologies, with the trained $\dt$-embedding creating vulnerability through at least three independent mechanisms: value-driven collapse (monotonic, late-onset, and SGS-resistant CIVO), entropy-driven instability (IRD collapse), and gradient-mediated effects (gradient starvation, SGS-paradoxical worsening).

645	\subsection{Extended Training Dynamics}\label{sec:extended_training}
646	
647	One Walker seed (s3, exp\_045) was extended from 112K to approximately 302K steps to investigate long-horizon CIVO dynamics. After onset (NVE peak $4.91\times$ at 132K steps), the system exhibited complete self-recovery: NVE declined to $0.05\times$ and evaluation performance recovered to 867.6 (94.7\% of baseline). This recovery required approximately 170K additional training steps beyond onset. The trajectory shows a single onset--recovery cycle with no recurrence, suggesting that the CIVO feedback loop can break spontaneously given sufficient training---though at substantial computational cost that the frozen $\dt$-embedding diagnostic eliminates entirely.
648	
649	\subsection{Why MPC Is Immune}
650	\label{sec:mpc_immune}
651	
652	\citet{nhu2025tawm} show that $\dt$-conditioning benefits TD-MPC2, raising the question of why it fails in DreamerV3.  The critical difference is that MPC re-plans from real states at every step, so imagination errors do not accumulate into the training signal because there is no critic bootstrapping from imagined trajectories.  In DreamerV3, the actor-critic trains entirely within imagination, and any systematic reward-prediction bias at $\dt > 1$ is amplified through the closed actor-critic loop.
653	
654	\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\dt$}{dt}-Embedding Dilemma}
655	\label{sec:embedding_dilemma}
656	
657	The factorial results reveal a fundamental tension: removing the $\dt$-embedding (008f) eliminates CIVO but at a $13\times$ cost to reward prediction quality ($0.7469$ vs.\ $0.0568$ MAE), as the world model cannot distinguish temporal scales.  Restricting imagination to $\dt{=}1$ while retaining the trained embedding (008e\_v3) avoids collapse in 2 of 4 seeds (50\%), while the remaining two collapse via distinct mechanisms: Pathway~A (CIVO-driven, entropy decrease; s3, NVE $4.02\times$) and Pathway~B (entropy-driven, entropy increase; s2, NVE $3.78\times$; \S\ref{sec:f6}).  Neither approach alone provides a complete solution.
658	
659	\subsection{Multiple Vulnerability Pathways}
660	\label{sec:pathways}
661	
662	The two collapse patterns identified in \S\ref{sec:f6}---CIVO (Pathway~A, value-driven, entropy decreasing) and a tentative second mode (Pathway~B, eval-driven, entropy increasing)---have distinct diagnostic signatures. Pathway~B is observed in only two seeds (008e\_v3\_s2 and 038\_s2); we present it as preliminary evidence that the trained $\dt$-embedding's learned features may affect both reward prediction across temporal scales (Pathway~A) and the representation space in which the actor operates (Pathway~B), though additional data is needed to confirm this as a distinct mechanism.  This explains why $\nve$ is sensitive but not perfectly specific: Pathway~B's late evaluation decline inflates $\nve$ through the same arithmetic as CIVO, yielding the sole false positive ($3.78\times$ for 008e\_v3 s2).  Distinguishing the two requires examining the temporal profile and entropy direction of collapse (Figure~\ref{fig:multiple_pathways}).
663	
664	
665	\subsection{Implications for Temporal Abstraction in World Models}
666	\label{sec:implications}
667	
668	CIVO identifies a structural incompatibility between flat temporal conditioning and imagination-based actor-critic training.  Hierarchical approaches (THICK \citep{gumbsch2024thick}, Hieros \citep{mattes2024hieros}, LS-Imagine \citep{li2025lsimagine}) may avoid this by maintaining separate dynamics per temporal scale.  Whether CIVO transfers to transformer-based world models (DreamerV4; \citealt{hafner2025dreamerv4}) depends on whether attention weights co-adapt with temporal embeddings analogously.  The CIVO mechanism requires three structural properties: (1) a temporal embedding that co-adapts with dynamics via gradient flow, (2) multi-step imagined rollouts used for critic training, and (3) a reward head that inherits latent biases from the shared representation.  Transformer-based world models that share these properties may exhibit analogous vulnerability; architectures that decouple temporal conditioning from the reward prediction pathway would not.
669

708	\paragraph{Cheetah Run $\sigma{=}0.5$: transient spike with permanent eval damage.}
709	Cheetah $\sigma{=}0.5$ exhibits a novel failure mode: a transient CIVO spike (NVE $= 10.67\times$ at 55K) followed by NVE recovery ($< 0.3\times$ by 95K), but permanent evaluation damage (26\% of baseline). The value signal self-corrects while the policy does not recover, suggesting that CIVO-induced representational damage during the spike persists within the observation window even when the critic subsequently recalibrates. This trajectory parallels the obs-decoder SGS seed~3 pattern (\S\ref{sec:seed_divergence}), where partial policy recovery coexists with persistent critic damage.
710	
711	\paragraph{Cheetah Run $\sigma{=}0.2$: insufficient dose.}
712	At $\sigma{=}0.2$, Cheetah reaches NVE $= 1.9\times$ at 55K---exceeding the $1.5\times$ threshold---consistent with Cheetah's higher baseline CIVO severity ($8$--$108\times$ vs.\ Walker's $2$--$6\times$). The noise magnitude that suffices for Walker is inadequate for Cheetah, but increasing $\sigma$ to 0.5 trades CIVO prevention for a different failure mode rather than achieving mitigation.
713	
714	\paragraph{PickCube-v1 $\sigma{=}0.2$: manipulation failure.}
715	PickCube reaches NVE $= 3.71\times$ at 52.5K with $\sigma{=}0.2$, indicating that TCN provides no protection in manipulation tasks. The combination of contact-gated dynamics and temporal conditioning may amplify the fidelity gap beyond what representation-level noise can attenuate.
716	
717	\paragraph{Dose-response analysis.}
718	We observe $\sigma{=}0.2$ as a local optimum on Walker Walk, achieving complete CIVO suppression in 2 of 4 seeds. Seeds~2 and~3 both fail but via distinct CIVO onset dynamics: seed~2 shows gradual escalation (NVE $= 2.11\times$ at 95K, still rising, eval ${\sim}5\%$), while seed~3 exhibits a phase-transition-like onset---NVE declined to $0.67\times$ at 95K before spiking to $3.04\times$ at 105K (eval 349.6 $\to$ 71.8 in 10K steps). When TCN fails to prevent CIVO onset, the resulting trajectory qualitatively resembles untreated CIVO in its terminal outcome---runaway NVE and eval collapse. The two failure modes (gradual escalation vs.\ sudden onset after apparent suppression) reinforce that TCN operates as a binary gate on CIVO onset rather than providing graded attenuation. Below the optimum, we observe $\sigma{=}0.1$ as insufficient (NVE $= 1.02\times$---borderline, eval 55\%); above it, $\sigma{=}0.5$ overdoses (oscillatory CIVO, eval $\leq 41\%$). Cross-seed variance at $\sigma{=}0.2$ (eval 5--91\%) exceeds the cross-dose difference by a wide margin, reinforcing that seed effects dominate dose effects. TCN is the only tested intervention achieving complete CIVO suppression in any configuration, but its high seed sensitivity and failure to transfer across environments position it as a \emph{diagnostic probe}---identifying the representation level as the relevant intervention target---rather than a deployable mitigation.
719	
720	\paragraph{Cheetah Run ``impossible dose'' dilemma.}
721	Exp\_030c (224K steps) reveals a qualitatively different dose-response on Cheetah. At $\sigma{=}1.0$, CIVO is eliminated (NVE${\leq}0.30$ throughout) and initial learning is \emph{unimpaired}: peak eval reaches 372 at 35K (116\% of baseline), demonstrating that $\sigma{=}1.0$ noise does not prevent policy acquisition. However, cumulative actor gradient interference produces progressive policy erosion---eval declines from 372 to 47 by 215K (15\% of baseline)---while the critic remains well-calibrated (value\_mean${=}12.4$, NVE${=}0.26$ at 215K). This establishes that \emph{policy degradation $\neq$ value corruption}: accurate value signals are insufficient when the actor cannot optimize effectively in noise-perturbed imagination.
722	
723	This ``correct values, failed policy'' pattern provides indirect evidence that CIVO is mechanistically distinct from standard overestimation. Double Q-learning and ensemble methods address critic estimation error, but at $\sigma{=}1.0$ the critic is accurate and the policy still collapses---a failure mode orthogonal to what these countermeasures target. The impossible dose dilemma scales with environment complexity: Cheetah's baseline CIVO severity ($8$--$108\times$ NVE) requires $\sigma \geq 1.0$ to suppress, but this exceeds the actor's gradient signal-to-noise tolerance. On Walker, a narrow effective window exists around $\sigma{=}0.2$; on Cheetah, the effective dose ($\sigma \geq 1.0$) exceeds the tolerable dose, and environments with higher CIVO severity will systematically lack a viable dose window.
724

779	\section{Online CIVO Detector Details}
780	\label{sec:detector_appendix}
781	
782	This appendix provides the full algorithm specification and validation results for the online CIVO detector described in \S\ref{sec:detector}.
783	
784	\subsection{Algorithm Specification}
785	
786	
787	The four conditions capture complementary signals: (1)~\emph{Magnitude} ($r_t \geq 0.49$) confirms that the value-to-evaluation ratio has reached a level indicative of overestimation; (2)~\emph{Velocity} ($\Delta r_t > 0.1$) rules out slow drift or plateau behavior; (3)~\emph{Acceleration} ($\Delta r_t > \Delta r_{t-1}$, both $> 0.1$) filters oscillatory paths; (4)~\emph{Evaluation decline} ($\geq 2$ of 3 recent checkpoints decline) confirms that overestimation coincides with actual performance degradation. A full comparison of window sizes appears in \S\ref{sec:appendix_sensitivity}.
788	
789	Sensitivity analysis over $\tau_\text{mag} \in [0.30, 0.70]$ confirms that $\tau_\text{mag} = 0.49$ maximizes Youden's $J = 0.714$; raising the threshold to $0.50$ loses one true positive (seed~3 triggers at $r_t = 0.497$) with no reduction in false positives. The velocity threshold $\tau_\text{vel} = 0.1$ is stable across $[0.05, 0.18]$ (TPR remains $5/5$).
790	
791	\subsection{Validation Results}
792	
793	\begin{table}[ht]
794	\centering
795	\caption{Retrospective CIVO detector validation ($n{=}14$). Detection type: ``predictive'' = alert precedes 20\% eval decline; ``reactive'' = alert follows decline onset.}
796	\label{tab:detector-validation}
797	\footnotesize
798	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
799	\begin{tabular}{llccccc}
800	\toprule
801	\textbf{Exp} & \textbf{Config} & \textbf{Verdict} & \textbf{Trigger} & \textbf{Type} & \textbf{Peak Eval} & $r_\text{ve}$ \\
802	\midrule
803	008d s0 & trained $\dt$-emb & TP & 45K & predict. & 167 & 0.69 \\
804	010b & trained $\dt$-emb (Cheetah) & TP & 65K & reactive & 322 & 3.18 \\
805	008d s3 & trained $\dt$-emb & TP & 45K & predict. & 452 & 0.50 \\
806	008d s4 & trained $\dt$-emb & TP & 105K & reactive & 383 & 3.03 \\
807	010b s0 & trained $\dt$-emb (Cheetah) & TP & 95K & reactive & 248 & 6.34 \\
808	\midrule
809	008f s3 & no $\dt$-emb & TN & -- & -- & 594 & -- \\
810	009b s2 & trained $\dt$-emb (self-corr.) & TN & -- & -- & 279 & -- \\
811	baseline s1 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 970 & -- \\
812	009d s0 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 951 & -- \\
813	009d s1 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 920 & -- \\
814	019b & trained $\dt$-emb (PickCube) & TN & -- & -- & 26.6 & -- \\

819	009c s3 & no $\dt$-emb & TN & -- & -- & 457 & -- \\
820	\midrule

822	\bottomrule
823	\end{tabular}
824	\end{table}
825	
826	The detector identifies all five CIVO-positive runs (TPR = 5/5). Two are detected predictively (008d seeds~0 and~3, both at 45K). Two false positives occur among nine negative cases (FPR = 2/9, precision 71\%, F$_1$ = 0.83). Run 009c develops genuine late-stage value overestimation unrelated to CIVO; run 009a triggers during a transient eval dip at 85K before recovery to 966. Early alerts (before 70K) carry higher diagnostic confidence.
827	
828	\subsection{Cross-Simulator Specificity (PickCube)}
829	
830	Exp\_019b applies the CIVO-trigger configuration to PickCube-v1 in ManiSkill3, a dense-reward manipulation task. The V/E ratio exhibits an oscillatory pattern rather than monotonic divergence: it spikes to $r_t = 5.66$ at 52.5K, then deflates to 1.38 by 72.5K. This trajectory prevents the acceleration condition from co-occurring with evaluation decline at any single checkpoint. Across all 14 checkpoints through 132.5K, the detector correctly remains silent (TN), confirming that the acceleration filter distinguishes transient spikes from CIVO's sustained monotonic divergence. A frozen-embedding control (exp\_054) confirms this classification: frozen PickCube-v1 exhibits a comparable burst (peak $8.81\times$ at 62.5K) that resolves within a single evaluation cycle ($0.55\times$ at 72.5K), establishing environment-specific origin independent of $\dt$-embedding training.
831	
832	\subsection{Detector Limitations}
833	
834	Four constraints bound the current detector. First, the three-checkpoint window cannot distinguish transient dips from sustained collapse. Second, the acceleration condition assumes monotonically increasing overestimation during onset. Third, the detector identifies a \emph{pattern} rather than a \emph{mechanism}: run 009c shows that non-CIVO pathologies produce the same signature. Fourth, threshold values were calibrated on fourteen runs across three environments; generalization to different reward scales requires further validation.
835	
836	\section{Dual-Critic Mechanism Ablation}
837	\label{sec:dual_critic_appendix}
838	
839	\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$2{\times}2$}{2x2} Factorial Design}
840	
841	We ablate the critic architecture in a $2{\times}2$ factorial: \{single, dual\} $\times$ \{narrow (${\sim}1.2$M), wide (${\sim}2.4$M)\} parameters. Table~\ref{tab:dual_critic_full} reports all configurations.
842

846	\label{tab:dual_critic_full}

855	035 s0 & Narrow dual (min) & 1.2M & 2.75$\times$ & 697.8 & CIVO $\to$ recovery \\
856	039 s1 & Narrow dual (min) & 1.2M & 2.19$\times$ & 592.7 & CIVO $\to$ recovery \\

868	{\raggedright\footnotesize $^*$CIVO prevented (NVE $\leq 0.3\times$) but non-CIVO eval collapse: eval peaked at 354 (155K), then declined to 120.9 (224K) with actor entropy increasing from $-3.59$ to $-0.45$.\par}
869	\end{table}
870	
871	\subsection{Critic Convergence (\texorpdfstring{$v_1 \approx v_2$}{v1 ≈ v2})}
872	
873	Across all dual-critic configurations and environments, the two critics converge to near-identical value functions. On Walker Walk, Pearson $\rho > 0.999$ between $v_1$ and $v_2$ outputs; on Cheetah Run and PickCube-v1, the gap is $< 0.2$ (exact match within numerical precision). Per-sample pessimism $\mathbb{E}[\max(v_1, v_2) - \min(v_1, v_2)]$ is 3--4\% of the theoretical bound for independent critics.
874	
875	This convergence occurs because both critics train on the same RSSM-generated latent features. Unlike model-free Double Q-learning, where each critic sees different mini-batches of real transitions and develops distinct function approximation errors, pure-imagination critics share identical inputs from the world model. The min operation therefore cannot provide the pessimism bias that makes Double Q effective in model-free settings.
876	
877	\subsection{Recovery vs.\ Prevention Mechanism}
878	
879	The $2{\times}2$ factorial reveals two separable dimensions:
880	
881	\paragraph{Recovery (min-dominated).} The min operation is the primary factor enabling recovery from CIVO. Both narrow dual-critic seeds experience CIVO and recover fully: exp\_035 (s0, $\nve = 2.75\times$, eval $33.8 \to 697.8$, recovery ratio $20.6\times$) and exp\_039 (s1, $\nve = 2.19\times$, eval $35.9 \to 592.7$, recovery ratio $16.5\times$). Both outperform wide single-critic (exp\_033, 2.4M params, eval 407.9) despite $2\times$ fewer parameters. The min operation reduces both peak CIVO severity (2.19--2.75$\times$ vs.\ 4.75$\times$ for the comparable single-critic) and enables consistent full recovery across seeds.
882	
883	\paragraph{Prevention (min $+$ capacity synergy).} CIVO prevention (NVE remaining below the $1.5\times$ threshold) requires both the min operation and increased capacity: only the wide dual-critic (exp\_032, 3 seeds) achieves this. The mechanism for this synergy is an open question; we hypothesize that increased capacity improves value calibration quality while the min operation constrains the overestimation feedback loop during early training when the two critics briefly diverge.
884	
885	\paragraph{Cross-environment limits.} On Cheetah Run, the wide dual-critic (exp\_034) reduces NVE from $8.27\times$ (untreated baseline) to $1.75\times$ ($\geq 4\times$ reduction) but cannot prevent CIVO onset, and recovery reaches only 31\% of the pre-CIVO peak. This confirms that the min $+$ capacity synergy has environment-dependent limits: in high-CIVO-severity environments, architecture-level interventions attenuate but do not eliminate the pathology.
886

The observation decoder contributes the smallest share (16--22\\%), yet its removal (via Full-SGS) is necessary for CIVO mitigation.  This dissociation arises because the reward head's gradient, while large, does not encode $\\dt$-specific structural information: it adjusts $e_{\\Delta t}$ to minimize a scalar prediction error that is largely $\\dt$-invariant. The reward head's large gradient contribution reflects sensitivity to $e_{\\Delta t}$'s parameters but not causal responsibility for the fidelity gap.\n\n\\subsubsection{Entropy Dynamics Across Configurations}\n\\label{sec:entropy_dynamics}\n\n\\begin{figure}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_entropy_dynamics.pdf}\n\\caption{Actor entropy trajectories for three configurations. All start at ${\\sim}8$ nats and converge to similar values (${\\sim}{-}4.5$ to ${-}5$). The CIVO-triggering configuration (008d, red) follows a normal entropy reduction trajectory; the divergence from the control (008f) is subtle in entropy space, consistent with CIVO operating through the value channel rather than the entropy channel. 018v2 (Full-SGS, seed~1) shows slightly lower terminal entropy.}\n\\label{fig:entropy_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsubsection{Exp\\_016e Reinterpreted as Stochastic Variation}\n\\label{sec:016e_reinterp}\n\nThe gradient decomposition establishes that exp\\_016e targets the largest gradient pathway (reward: 38--49\\%) but not the informationally critical one (observation decoders: 16--22\\%).  The apparent timing differences between exp\\_016e and exp\\_008d (later collapse onset, 75K vs.\\ 25K, and higher peak evaluation, 326.7 vs.\\ 167.2) must be attributed to stochastic variation rather than SGS effects, since the critical observation decoder channel remains intact.  All three trajectories (exp\\_008d, exp\\_009b, exp\\_016e) share the invariant identified by the NVE diagnostic: each crosses the $1.5\\times$ pathological threshold at some point during training.\n\n\\subsection{Post-CIVO Outcomes: Why Seeds Diverge}\n\\label{sec:seed_divergence}\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Post-CIVO outcomes for all seven CIVO-positive seeds across two environments. Peak/Final NVE measured in the 35K+ window.}\n\\label{tab:post_civo}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccl}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Env} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Peak} & \\textbf{Final} & \\textbf{Eval} & \\textbf{Outcome} \\\\\n\\midrule\nWalker & s1 (008d) & 4.12$\\times$ & 4.12$\\times$ & 82.8 & Full collapse \\\\\nWalker & s2 (009b) & 1.62$\\times$ & 0.38$\\times$ & 267.9 & Transient CIVO \\\\\nWalker & s3 (008d\\_s3) & 1.53$\\times$ & 1.53$\\times$ & 268$^\\diamond$ & Late-onset collapse \\\\\nWalker & s4 (008d\\_s4) & 4.62$\\times$ & 4.62$\\times$ & 63.6 & Full collapse \\\\\nWalker & s5 (059) & 4.72$\\times$ & 1.22$\\times$ & 95.8 & Collapse$^\\dagger$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nCheetah & s1 (010b) & 8.27$\\times$ & 8.27$\\times$ & 51.7 & Full collapse \\\\\nCheetah & s0 (010b\\_s0) & 108.52$\\times$ & 108.52$\\times$ & 0.83 & Full collapse \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\raggedright\\footnotesize $^\\diamond$ Transitional; train returns still declining at 105K. $^\\dagger$~NVE self-corrected ($4.72\\times$ $\\to$ $1.22\\times$) but policy did not recover (eval 10.5\\% of baseline).\\par}\n\\end{table}\n\nAll seven CIVO-positive seeds cross the $\\nve > 1.5\\times$ threshold (Table~\\ref{tab:post_civo}), yet the downstream damage is stochastic.\\footnote{Exp\\_009e was excluded from multi-seed analyses due to a seed configuration error (default seed=0, identical to exp\\_008d).}  Full collapse is the majority outcome (6/7 seeds), with one self-correction case (Walker seed 2); seed 5 exemplifies a dissociation where NVE self-corrects ($4.72\\times \\to 1.22\\times$) while the policy remains collapsed (eval 10.5\\% of baseline). Cheetah seeds exhibit more extreme overestimation (peak NVE $8.27$--$108.52\\times$ vs.\\ Walker's $1.53$--$4.62\\times$), consistent with the Cheetah reward scale amplifying the fidelity gap.  We hypothesize that the divergence relates to the critic's representational capacity at the time of CIVO onset.  \\citet{lyle2022capacity} demonstrate that deep RL networks suffer progressive capacity loss, in which early-learned features become increasingly difficult to overwrite.  If the critic's capacity is already partially consumed when CIVO injects overestimation, the network cannot recalibrate (seed~0); if sufficient capacity remains, the critic can absorb transient overestimation and recover (seed~2).\n\n\\paragraph{A fourth trajectory: CIVO-induced persistent value collapse.}\nObs-decoder SGS seed~3 (exp\\_025) exhibits a trajectory not observed in the untreated seeds: healthy through 105K ($\\nve < 1.0\\times$, eval 818.6), CIVO onset at 135K ($\\nve = 5.47\\times$, eval 67.0), followed by evaluation recovery (590.2 at 215K, 64\\% of baseline) but persistent value collapse ($\\nve = 0.14\\times$, value mean 80.6). The critic undershoots after the CIVO episode, suggesting that CIVO-induced representational damage persists even when the policy partially recovers. This contrasts with transient CIVO (seed~2, 009b), where both value and evaluation recover jointly, and with full collapse (seed~1, 008d), where neither recovers. Seed~4 exhibits the same terminal value collapse ($\\nve = 0.14\\times$ at 215K, eval 242), suggesting this overcorrection is systematic rather than seed-specific. The pattern adds a fourth post-CIVO outcome---policy recovery with critic damage---to the three documented in Table~\\ref{tab:post_civo}.\n\n\\subsubsection{Failure and Degradation Mode Taxonomy}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:failure_modes} classifies the seven distinct failure and degradation modes observed across all experimental configurations. The taxonomy reveals that CIVO is not a single failure mode but a family of related pathologies, with the trained $\\dt$-embedding creating vulnerability through at least three independent mechanisms: value-driven collapse (monotonic, late-onset, and SGS-resistant CIVO), entropy-driven instability (IRD collapse), and gradient-mediated effects (gradient starvation, SGS-paradoxical worsening).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Failure and degradation mode taxonomy. Each mode is characterized by its NVE trajectory, entropy behavior, and primary mechanism.}\n\\label{tab:failure_modes}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Representative} & \\textbf{NVE} & \\textbf{Entropy} & \\textbf{Mechanism} \\\\\n\\midrule\nMonotonic CIVO & 008d, 008d\\_s4, 010b\\_s0 & $\\uparrow$ sustained & $\\downarrow$ & Reward bias $\\to$ critic $\\to$ actor \\\\\nLate-onset CIVO & 008d\\_s3 & $\\uparrow$ delayed & $\\downarrow$ & Same, delayed onset \\\\\nTransient CIVO & 009b & Peak $\\to$ $\\downarrow$ & Stable & Self-correction via capacity \\\\\nIRD collapse & 008e\\_v3\\_s2 & $\\uparrow$ late & $\\uparrow$ & Entropy-driven, values stable \\\\\nGrad.\\ starvation & 015b & Oscillates, decays & Stable & Frozen emb $\\to$ zero actor grad \\\\\nSGS-resistant & 018\\_s2 & $\\uparrow$ & $\\downarrow$ & Forward-pass conditioning \\\\\nSGS-paradoxical & 016e & $\\uparrow$ sustained & $\\downarrow$ & Reward SGS $\\to$ obs-dec reliance \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Extended Training Dynamics}\\label{sec:extended_training}\n\nOne Walker seed (s3, exp\\_045) was extended from 112K to approximately 302K steps to investigate long-horizon CIVO dynamics. After onset (NVE peak $4.91\\times$ at 132K steps), the system exhibited complete self-recovery: NVE declined to $0.05\\times$ and evaluation performance recovered to 867.6 (94.7\\% of baseline). This recovery required approximately 170K additional training steps beyond onset. The trajectory shows a single onset--recovery cycle with no recurrence, suggesting that the CIVO feedback loop can break spontaneously given sufficient training---though at substantial computational cost that the frozen $\\dt$-embedding diagnostic eliminates entirely.\n\n\\subsection{Why MPC Is Immune}\n\\label{sec:mpc_immune}\n\n\\citet{nhu2025tawm} show that $\\dt$-conditioning benefits TD-MPC2, raising the question of why it fails in DreamerV3.  The critical difference is that MPC re-plans from real states at every step, so imagination errors do not accumulate into the training signal because there is no critic bootstrapping from imagined trajectories.  In DreamerV3, the actor-critic trains entirely within imagination, and any systematic reward-prediction bias at $\\dt > 1$ is amplified through the closed actor-critic loop.\n\n\\subsection{The \\texorpdfstring{$\\dt$}{dt}-Embedding Dilemma}\n\\label{sec:embedding_dilemma}\n\nThe factorial results reveal a fundamental tension: removing the $\\dt$-embedding (008f) eliminates CIVO but at a $13\\times$ cost to reward prediction quality ($0.7469$ vs.\\ $0.0568$ MAE), as the world model cannot distinguish temporal scales.  Restricting imagination to $\\dt{=}1$ while retaining the trained embedding (008e\\_v3) avoids collapse in 2 of 4 seeds (50\\%), while the remaining two collapse via distinct mechanisms: Pathway~A (CIVO-driven, entropy decrease; s3, NVE $4.02\\times$) and Pathway~B (entropy-driven, entropy increase; s2, NVE $3.78\\times$; \\S\\ref{sec:f6}).  Neither approach alone provides a complete solution.\n\n\\subsection{Multiple Vulnerability Pathways}\n\\label{sec:pathways}\n\nThe two collapse patterns identified in \\S\\ref{sec:f6}---CIVO (Pathway~A, value-driven, entropy decreasing) and a tentative second mode (Pathway~B, eval-driven, entropy increasing)---have distinct diagnostic signatures. Pathway~B is observed in only two seeds (008e\\_v3\\_s2 and 038\\_s2); we present it as preliminary evidence that the trained $\\dt$-embedding's learned features may affect both reward prediction across temporal scales (Pathway~A) and the representation space in which the actor operates (Pathway~B), though additional data is needed to confirm this as a distinct mechanism.  This explains why $\\nve$ is sensitive but not perfectly specific: Pathway~B's late evaluation decline inflates $\\nve$ through the same arithmetic as CIVO, yielding the sole false positive ($3.78\\times$ for 008e\\_v3 s2).  Distinguishing the two requires examining the temporal profile and entropy direction of collapse (Figure~\\ref{fig:multiple_pathways}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Implications for Temporal Abstraction in World Models}\n\\label{sec:implications}\n\nCIVO identifies a structural incompatibility between flat temporal conditioning and imagination-based actor-critic training.  Hierarchical approaches (THICK \\citep{gumbsch2024thick}, Hieros \\citep{mattes2024hieros}, LS-Imagine \\citep{li2025lsimagine}) may avoid this by maintaining separate dynamics per temporal scale.  Whether CIVO transfers to transformer-based world models (DreamerV4; \\citealt{hafner2025dreamerv4}) depends on whether attention weights co-adapt with temporal embeddings analogously.  The CIVO mechanism requires three structural properties: (1) a temporal embedding that co-adapts with dynamics via gradient flow, (2) multi-step imagined rollouts used for critic training, and (3) a reward head that inherits latent biases from the shared representation.  Transformer-based world models that share these properties may exhibit analogous vulnerability; architectures that decouple temporal conditioning from the reward prediction pathway would not.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Environment TCN Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:tcn_cross_env}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:tcn_full} reports the full TCN evaluation across environments and noise magnitudes.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{TCN results across environments and noise magnitudes. Prevention = peak NVE $< 1.5\\times$ (35K+).}\n\\label{tab:tcn_full}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{4pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Env} & $\\sigma$ & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{NVE$_{\\max}$} & \\textbf{Prevented?} & \\textbf{Eval (\\%)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nWalker & 0.1 & s0 & 1.02 & \\checkmark$^*$ & 55\\% \\\\\nWalker & 0.2 & s0 & 0.91 & \\checkmark & 91\\% \\\\\nWalker & 0.2 & s1 & 0.64 & \\checkmark & 45\\% \\\\\nWalker & 0.2 & s2 & 2.11$^\\dagger$ & $\\times$ & ${\\sim}5\\%$ \\\\\n\\rowcolor{gray!15}\nWalker & 0.2 & s3 & 3.04$^\\P$ & $\\times$ & 11\\% \\\\\nWalker & 0.5 & s0 & 3.97 & $\\times$ & 27\\% \\\\\nWalker & 0.5 & s1 & 1.64 & $\\times$ & 41\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nCheetah & 0.2 & s0 & 1.90 & $\\times$ & crashed \\\\\nCheetah & 0.5 & s0 & 10.67 & $\\times$ & 26\\% \\\\\nCheetah & 1.0 & s0 & $\\leq$0.30 & \\checkmark$^\\S$ & 15\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\nPickCube & 0.2 & s0 & 3.71 & $\\times$ & failing \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\raggedright\\footnotesize $^*$Borderline: NVE${=}1.02\\times$ at 95K, below $1.5\\times$ threshold but above $1.0\\times$.\\\\\n$^\\dagger$NVE $= 2.11\\times$ at 95K, still rising; eval ${\\sim}5\\%$ of baseline.\\\\\n$^\\P$Late-onset CIVO: NVE declined to $0.67\\times$ at 95K before phase-transition-like spike to $3.04\\times$ at 105K; eval 349.6 at 95K $\\to$ 71.8 at 105K $\\to$ 104.5 at 215K.\\\\\n$^\\S$CIVO prevented (NVE${\\leq}0.30$); peak eval 116\\% baseline at 35K, declining to 15\\% by 215K via progressive policy erosion. See ``impossible dose'' discussion.\\par}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Cheetah Run $\\sigma{=}0.5$: transient spike with permanent eval damage.}\nCheetah $\\sigma{=}0.5$ exhibits a novel failure mode: a transient CIVO spike (NVE $= 10.67\\times$ at 55K) followed by NVE recovery ($< 0.3\\times$ by 95K), but permanent evaluation damage (26\\% of baseline). The value signal self-corrects while the policy does not recover, suggesting that CIVO-induced representational damage during the spike persists within the observation window even when the critic subsequently recalibrates. This trajectory parallels the obs-decoder SGS seed~3 pattern (\\S\\ref{sec:seed_divergence}), where partial policy recovery coexists with persistent critic damage.\n\n\\paragraph{Cheetah Run $\\sigma{=}0.2$: insufficient dose.}\nAt $\\sigma{=}0.2$, Cheetah reaches NVE $= 1.9\\times$ at 55K---exceeding the $1.5\\times$ threshold---consistent with Cheetah's higher baseline CIVO severity ($8$--$108\\times$ vs.\\ Walker's $2$--$6\\times$). The noise magnitude that suffices for Walker is inadequate for Cheetah, but increasing $\\sigma$ to 0.5 trades CIVO prevention for a different failure mode rather than achieving mitigation.\n\n\\paragraph{PickCube-v1 $\\sigma{=}0.2$: manipulation failure.}\nPickCube reaches NVE $= 3.71\\times$ at 52.5K with $\\sigma{=}0.2$, indicating that TCN provides no protection in manipulation tasks. The combination of contact-gated dynamics and temporal conditioning may amplify the fidelity gap beyond what representation-level noise can attenuate.\n\n\\paragraph{Dose-response analysis.}\nWe observe $\\sigma{=}0.2$ as a local optimum on Walker Walk, achieving complete CIVO suppression in 2 of 4 seeds. Seeds~2 and~3 both fail but via distinct CIVO onset dynamics: seed~2 shows gradual escalation (NVE $= 2.11\\times$ at 95K, still rising, eval ${\\sim}5\\%$), while seed~3 exhibits a phase-transition-like onset---NVE declined to $0.67\\times$ at 95K before spiking to $3.04\\times$ at 105K (eval 349.6 $\\to$ 71.8 in 10K steps). When TCN fails to prevent CIVO onset, the resulting trajectory qualitatively resembles untreated CIVO in its terminal outcome---runaway NVE and eval collapse. The two failure modes (gradual escalation vs.\\ sudden onset after apparent suppression) reinforce that TCN operates as a binary gate on CIVO onset rather than providing graded attenuation. Below the optimum, we observe $\\sigma{=}0.1$ as insufficient (NVE $= 1.02\\times$---borderline, eval 55\\%); above it, $\\sigma{=}0.5$ overdoses (oscillatory CIVO, eval $\\leq 41\\%$). Cross-seed variance at $\\sigma{=}0.2$ (eval 5--91\\%) exceeds the cross-dose difference by a wide margin, reinforcing that seed effects dominate dose effects. TCN is the only tested intervention achieving complete CIVO suppression in any configuration, but its high seed sensitivity and failure to transfer across environments position it as a \\emph{diagnostic probe}---identifying the representation level as the relevant intervention target---rather than a deployable mitigation.\n\n\\paragraph{Cheetah Run ``impossible dose'' dilemma.}\nExp\\_030c (224K steps) reveals a qualitatively different dose-response on Cheetah. At $\\sigma{=}1.0$, CIVO is eliminated (NVE${\\leq}0.30$ throughout) and initial learning is \\emph{unimpaired}: peak eval reaches 372 at 35K (116\\% of baseline), demonstrating that $\\sigma{=}1.0$ noise does not prevent policy acquisition. However, cumulative actor gradient interference produces progressive policy erosion---eval declines from 372 to 47 by 215K (15\\% of baseline)---while the critic remains well-calibrated (value\\_mean${=}12.4$, NVE${=}0.26$ at 215K). This establishes that \\emph{policy degradation $\\neq$ value corruption}: accurate value signals are insufficient when the actor cannot optimize effectively in noise-perturbed imagination.\n\nThis ``correct values, failed policy'' pattern provides indirect evidence that CIVO is mechanistically distinct from standard overestimation. Double Q-learning and ensemble methods address critic estimation error, but at $\\sigma{=}1.0$ the critic is accurate and the policy still collapses---a failure mode orthogonal to what these countermeasures target. The impossible dose dilemma scales with environment complexity: Cheetah's baseline CIVO severity ($8$--$108\\times$ NVE) requires $\\sigma \\geq 1.0$ to suppress, but this exceeds the actor's gradient signal-to-noise tolerance. On Walker, a narrow effective window exists around $\\sigma{=}0.2$; on Cheetah, the effective dose ($\\sigma \\geq 1.0$) exceeds the tolerable dose, and environments with higher CIVO severity will systematically lack a viable dose window.\n\n\\section{DreamerV3 Hyperparameters}\n\\label{sec:hyperparameters}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists the DreamerV3 hyperparameters used across all experiments. Unless otherwise noted, we use the default DreamerV3 configuration for continuous-control tasks (\\texttt{dmc\\_proprio}).\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\caption{DreamerV3 hyperparameters for all experiments. Modifications from defaults are marked with $\\diamond$.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{ll}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{\\textit{World Model}} \\\\\nRSSM discrete states & 32 classes $\\times$ 32 categories \\\\\nRSSM stochastic dim & 32 \\\\\nRSSM deterministic dim & 512 \\\\\nMLP hidden size & 512 \\\\\nMLP layers & 2 \\\\\nActivation & SiLU \\\\\nNormalization & LayerNorm \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{\\textit{Training}} \\\\\nBatch size & 16 sequences $\\times$ 64 steps \\\\\nLearning rate & $3 \\times 10^{-4}$ (Adam, $\\epsilon{=}10^{-8}$) \\\\\nSequence length & 64 \\\\\nTraining ratio & 512 \\\\\nFree nats & 1.0 \\\\\nKL balance ($\\alpha$) & 0.8 \\\\\nDiscount ($\\gamma$) & 0.997 \\\\\n$\\lambda$-return & 0.95 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{\\textit{Policy}} \\\\\nImagination horizon$^\\diamond$ & 8 or 15 (see Table~\\ref{tab:complete_results}) \\\\\nActor entropy scale & $3 \\times 10^{-4}$ \\\\\nActor gradient & Reinforce + dynamics backprop \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{\\textit{Temporal Abstraction}$^\\diamond$} \\\\\nGate enabled & True (all non-baseline) \\\\\nGate fixed $\\dt$ & 1 or 2 (per config) \\\\\nSubsample max $\\dt$ & 1 (OFF) or 2 (ON) \\\\\n$\\dt$-embedding dim & 32 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{l}{\\textit{Environment}} \\\\\nAction repeat & 2 (Walker Walk, Cheetah Run) \\\\\nObservation & Proprioceptive (no pixels) \\\\\nTotal environment steps & 105K \\\\\nEvaluation frequency & Every 10K steps \\\\\nEvaluation episodes & 10 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\section{Online CIVO Detector Details}\n\\label{sec:detector_appendix}\n\nThis appendix provides the full algorithm specification and validation results for the online CIVO detector described in \\S\\ref{sec:detector}.\n\n\\subsection{Algorithm Specification}\n\n\nThe four conditions capture complementary signals: (1)~\\emph{Magnitude} ($r_t \\geq 0.49$) confirms that the value-to-evaluation ratio has reached a level indicative of overestimation; (2)~\\emph{Velocity} ($\\Delta r_t > 0.1$) rules out slow drift or plateau behavior; (3)~\\emph{Acceleration} ($\\Delta r_t > \\Delta r_{t-1}$, both $> 0.1$) filters oscillatory paths; (4)~\\emph{Evaluation decline} ($\\geq 2$ of 3 recent checkpoints decline) confirms that overestimation coincides with actual performance degradation. A full comparison of window sizes appears in \\S\\ref{sec:appendix_sensitivity}.\n\nSensitivity analysis over $\\tau_\\text{mag} \\in [0.30, 0.70]$ confirms that $\\tau_\\text{mag} = 0.49$ maximizes Youden's $J = 0.714$; raising the threshold to $0.50$ loses one true positive (seed~3 triggers at $r_t = 0.497$) with no reduction in false positives. The velocity threshold $\\tau_\\text{vel} = 0.1$ is stable across $[0.05, 0.18]$ (TPR remains $5/5$).\n\n\\subsection{Validation Results}\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Retrospective CIVO detector validation ($n{=}14$). Detection type: ``predictive'' = alert precedes 20\\% eval decline; ``reactive'' = alert follows decline onset.}\n\\label{tab:detector-validation}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Exp} & \\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Verdict} & \\textbf{Trigger} & \\textbf{Type} & \\textbf{Peak Eval} & $r_\\text{ve}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n008d s0 & trained $\\dt$-emb & TP & 45K & predict. & 167 & 0.69 \\\\\n010b & trained $\\dt$-emb (Cheetah) & TP & 65K & reactive & 322 & 3.18 \\\\\n008d s3 & trained $\\dt$-emb & TP & 45K & predict. & 452 & 0.50 \\\\\n008d s4 & trained $\\dt$-emb & TP & 105K & reactive & 383 & 3.03 \\\\\n010b s0 & trained $\\dt$-emb (Cheetah) & TP & 95K & reactive & 248 & 6.34 \\\\\n\\midrule\n008f s3 & no $\\dt$-emb & TN & -- & -- & 594 & -- \\\\\n009b s2 & trained $\\dt$-emb (self-corr.) & TN & -- & -- & 279 & -- \\\\\nbaseline s1 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 970 & -- \\\\\n009d s0 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 951 & -- \\\\\n009d s1 & vanilla & TN & -- & -- & 920 & -- \\\\\n019b & trained $\\dt$-emb (PickCube) & TN & -- & -- & 26.6 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n009c s2 & no $\\dt$-emb & FP & 105K & reactive & 359 & 0.75 \\\\\n009a s2 & vanilla & FP & 85K & predict. & 967 & 0.73 \\\\\n\\midrule\n009c s3 & no $\\dt$-emb & TN & -- & -- & 457 & -- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{TPR = 5/5 (1.00), \\quad FPR = 2/9 (0.22)} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe detector identifies all five CIVO-positive runs (TPR = 5/5). Two are detected predictively (008d seeds~0 and~3, both at 45K). Two false positives occur among nine negative cases (FPR = 2/9, precision 71\\%, F$_1$ = 0.83). Run 009c develops genuine late-stage value overestimation unrelated to CIVO; run 009a triggers during a transient eval dip at 85K before recovery to 966. Early alerts (before 70K) carry higher diagnostic confidence.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Simulator Specificity (PickCube)}\n\nExp\\_019b applies the CIVO-trigger configuration to PickCube-v1 in ManiSkill3, a dense-reward manipulation task. The V/E ratio exhibits an oscillatory pattern rather than monotonic divergence: it spikes to $r_t = 5.66$ at 52.5K, then deflates to 1.38 by 72.5K. This trajectory prevents the acceleration condition from co-occurring with evaluation decline at any single checkpoint. Across all 14 checkpoints through 132.5K, the detector correctly remains silent (TN), confirming that the acceleration filter distinguishes transient spikes from CIVO's sustained monotonic divergence. A frozen-embedding control (exp\\_054) confirms this classification: frozen PickCube-v1 exhibits a comparable burst (peak $8.81\\times$ at 62.5K) that resolves within a single evaluation cycle ($0.55\\times$ at 72.5K), establishing environment-specific origin independent of $\\dt$-embedding training.\n\n\\subsection{Detector Limitations}\n\nFour constraints bound the current detector. First, the three-checkpoint window cannot distinguish transient dips from sustained collapse. Second, the acceleration condition assumes monotonically increasing overestimation during onset. Third, the detector identifies a \\emph{pattern} rather than a \\emph{mechanism}: run 009c shows that non-CIVO pathologies produce the same signature. Fourth, threshold values were calibrated on fourteen runs across three environments; generalization to different reward scales requires further validation.\n\n\\section{Dual-Critic Mechanism Ablation}\n\\label{sec:dual_critic_appendix}\n\n\\subsection{\\texorpdfstring{$2{\\times}2$}{2x2} Factorial Design}\n\nWe ablate the critic architecture in a $2{\\times}2$ factorial: \\{single, dual\\} $\\times$ \\{narrow (${\\sim}1.2$M), wide (${\\sim}2.4$M)\\} parameters. Table~\\ref{tab:dual_critic_full} reports all configurations.\n\n\\begin{table}[ht]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Dual-critic $2{\\times}2$ factorial results. Walker Walk unless noted.}\n\\label{tab:dual_critic_full}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcccl}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Exp} & \\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{NVE} & \\textbf{Eval} & \\textbf{Outcome} \\\\\n\\midrule\n008d & Single narrow & 1.2M & 4.12$\\times$ & 82.8 & Collapse \\\\\n033 & Wide single & 2.4M & 4.75$\\times$ & 407.9 & CIVO $\\to$ self-repair \\\\\n035 s0 & Narrow dual (min) & 1.2M & 2.75$\\times$ & 697.8 & CIVO $\\to$ recovery \\\\\n039 s1 & Narrow dual (min) & 1.2M & 2.19$\\times$ & 592.7 & CIVO $\\to$ recovery \\\\\n032 s0 & Wide dual (min) & 2.4M & $\\leq$1.1$\\times$ & 739.7 & CIVO prevented \\\\\n032b s1 & Wide dual (min) & 2.4M & 1.4$\\times$ & 551.8 & CIVO prevented \\\\\n038 s2 & Wide dual (min) & 2.4M & $\\leq$0.3$\\times$ & 120.9$^*$ & Non-CIVO collapse \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{Cross-environment}} \\\\\n034 & Cheetah dual & 2.4M & 1.75$\\times$ & 97.5 & Partial recovery (31\\%) \\\\\n037 & Cheetah wide single & 2.4M & 3.60$\\times$ & 146.1 & CIVO $\\to$ self-repair \\\\\n036 & PickCube dual & 2.4M & 2.3$\\times$ & 27.7 & Transient CIVO \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\vspace{1pt}\n{\\raggedright\\footnotesize $^*$CIVO prevented (NVE $\\leq 0.3\\times$) but non-CIVO eval collapse: eval peaked at 354 (155K), then declined to 120.9 (224K) with actor entropy increasing from $-3.59$ to $-0.45$.\\par}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Critic Convergence (\\texorpdfstring{$v_1 \\approx v_2$}{v1 ≈ v2})}\n\nAcross all dual-critic configurations and environments, the two critics converge to near-identical value functions. On Walker Walk, Pearson $\\rho > 0.999$ between $v_1$ and $v_2$ outputs; on Cheetah Run and PickCube-v1, the gap is $< 0.2$ (exact match within numerical precision). Per-sample pessimism $\\mathbb{E}[\\max(v_1, v_2) - \\min(v_1, v_2)]$ is 3--4\\% of the theoretical bound for independent critics.\n\nThis convergence occurs because both critics train on the same RSSM-generated latent features. Unlike model-free Double Q-learning, where each critic sees different mini-batches of real transitions and develops distinct function approximation errors, pure-imagination critics share identical inputs from the world model. The min operation therefore cannot provide the pessimism bias that makes Double Q effective in model-free settings.\n\n\\subsection{Recovery vs.\\ Prevention Mechanism}\n\nThe $2{\\times}2$ factorial reveals two separable dimensions:\n\n\\paragraph{Recovery (min-dominated).} The min operation is the primary factor enabling recovery from CIVO. Both narrow dual-critic seeds experience CIVO and recover fully: exp\\_035 (s0, $\\nve = 2.75\\times$, eval $33.8 \\to 697.8$, recovery ratio $20.6\\times$) and exp\\_039 (s1, $\\nve = 2.19\\times$, eval $35.9 \\to 592.7$, recovery ratio $16.5\\times$). Both outperform wide single-critic (exp\\_033, 2.4M params, eval 407.9) despite $2\\times$ fewer parameters. The min operation reduces both peak CIVO severity (2.19--2.75$\\times$ vs.\\ 4.75$\\times$ for the comparable single-critic) and enables consistent full recovery across seeds.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevention (min $+$ capacity synergy).} CIVO prevention (NVE remaining below the $1.5\\times$ threshold) requires both the min operation and increased capacity: only the wide dual-critic (exp\\_032, 3 seeds) achieves this. The mechanism for this synergy is an open question; we hypothesize that increased capacity improves value calibration quality while the min operation constrains the overestimation feedback loop during early training when the two critics briefly diverge.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-environment limits.} On Cheetah Run, the wide dual-critic (exp\\_034) reduces NVE from $8.27\\times$ (untreated baseline) to $1.75\\times$ ($\\geq 4\\times$ reduction) but cannot prevent CIVO onset, and recovery reaches only 31\\% of the pre-CIVO peak. This confirms that the min $+$ capacity synergy has environment-dependent limits: in high-CIVO-severity environments, architecture-level interventions attenuate but do not eliminate the pathology.\n",
      "numLines": 387,
      "startLine": 500,
      "totalLines": 886
    }
  }
}
